    window.loadScript = (url, { charset = 'UTF-8', ...attributes } = {}, location = 'head') => {
      const existingScript = document.querySelector('script[src="' + url + '"]');
      if (existingScript) {
        return;
      }

      const script = document.createElement('script');
      script.src = url;
      script.type = 'text/javascript';

      script.async = true;
      script.charset = charset;

      if (attributes) {
        Object.entries(attributes).forEach(([key, value]) => {
          script.setAttribute(key, value);
        });
      }

      if (location === 'head') {
        document.head.appendChild(script);
      } else {
        document.body.appendChild(script);
      }

      return script;
    };

the timeline on which quantum attack might happen. Cloudflare is responding by moving our target for full post-quantum security to 2029.&quot;],&quot;featured&quot;:[0,false],&quot;html&quot;:[0,&quot;&lt;p&gt;Cloudflare is accelerating its post-quantum roadmap. We now target &lt;b&gt;2029&lt;/b&gt; to be fully post-quantum (PQ) secure including, crucially, post-quantum authentication.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;At Cloudflare, we believe in making the Internet private and secure by default. We started by offering &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/introducing-universal-ssl/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;free universal SSL certificates&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; in 2014, began preparing our &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/towards-post-quantum-cryptography-in-tls/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;post-quantum migration&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; in 2019, and enabled post-quantum encryption for &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/post-quantum-for-all/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;all websites&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; and APIs in 2022, mitigating harvest-now/decrypt-later attacks. While weâre excited by the fact that over &lt;a href=\&quot;https://radar.cloudflare.com/post-quantum\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;65% of human traffic&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to Cloudflare is post-quantum encrypted, our work is not done until authentication is also upgraded. Credible new research and rapid industry developments suggest that the deadline to migrate is much sooner than expected. This is a challenge that any organization must treat with urgency, which is why weâre expediting our own internal Q-Day readiness timeline.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;What happened? Last week, Google &lt;a href=\&quot;https://research.google/blog/safeguarding-cryptocurrency-by-disclosing-quantum-vulnerabilities-responsibly/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;announced&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; they had drastically improved upon the quantum algorithm to break elliptic curve cryptography, which is widely used to secure the Internet. They did not reveal the algorithm, but instead provided a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zero-knowledge_proof\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;zero-knowledge proof&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; that they have one.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This is not even the biggest breakthrough. That same day, Oratomic &lt;a href=\&quot;https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28627\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;published&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; a resource estimate for breaking RSA-2048 and P-256 on a neutral atom computer. For P-256, it only requires a shockingly low 10,000 qubits. Googleâs motivation behind their recent announcement to also pursue &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.google/innovation-and-ai/technology/research/neutral-atom-quantum-computers/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;neutral atoms&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; alongside superconducting quantum computers becomes clear now. Although Oratomic explains their basic approach, they still leave out crucial details &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.oratomic.com/news/responsible-disclosure-in-the-era-of-quantum-computers\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;on purpose&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;These independent advances prompted Google to accelerate their post-quantum migration timeline to &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.google/innovation-and-ai/technology/safety-security/cryptography-migration-timeline/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;2029&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Whatâs more, in their announcement and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://westerbaan.name/~bas/rwpqc2026/sophie.pdf\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;other talks&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, Google has placed a priority on quantum-secure authentication over mitigating harvest-now/decrypt-later attacks. As we discuss next, this priority indicates that Google is concerned about Q-Day coming as soon as 2030. Following the announcements, IBM Quantum Safeâs CTO is more pessimistic and canât rule out quantum âmoonshot attacksâ on high value targets &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/quantum-timeline-elliptic-curve-cryptography-just-jumped-osborne-k1oae\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;as early as 2029&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The quantum threat is well known: Q-Day is the day that sufficiently capable quantum computers can break essential cryptography used to protect data and access across systems today.&lt;i&gt; &lt;/i&gt;Cryptographically relevant quantum computers (CRQCs) donât exist yet, but many labs across the world are pursuing different approaches to building one. Until recently, progress on CRQCs has been mostly public, but there is no reason to expect that will continue. Indeed, there is ample reason to expect that progress will leave the public eye. As quantum computer scientist Scott Aaronson &lt;a href=\&quot;https://scottaaronson.blog/?p=9425\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;warned&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; at the end of 2025:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;blockquote&gt;&lt;p&gt;[A]t some point, the people doing detailed estimates of how many physical qubits and gates itâll take to break actually deployed cryptosystems using Shorâs algorithm are going to stop publishing those estimates, if for no other reason than the risk of giving too much information to adversaries. Indeed, for all we know, that point may have been passed already.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/blockquote&gt;&lt;p&gt;That point has now passed indeed.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;why-now-independent-progress-on-three-fronts\&quot;&gt;Why now: independent progress on three fronts&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#why-now-independent-progress-on-three-fronts\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Weâd like to spend some words on why itâs difficult to predict progress on quantum computing. Sudden âquantumâ leaps in understanding, like the one we witnessed last week, can occur even if everything happens in the public eye. Simply put, breaking cryptography with a quantum computer requires engineering on three independent fronts: quantum hardware, error correction, and quantum software. Progress on each front compounds progress on the others.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Hardware.&lt;/b&gt; There are many different competing approaches. We mentioned neutral atoms and superconducting qubits, but there are also ion-trap, photonics, and moonshots like topological qubits. Complementary approaches can even be &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.caltech.edu/about/news/low-noise-transducers-to-bridge-the-gap-between-microwave-and-optical-qubits\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;combined&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Most of these approaches are pursued by several labs around the world. They all have their distinct engineering challenges and problems to solve before they can scale up. A few years ago, all of them had a long list of open challenges, and it was unclear if any of them would scale. Today most of them have made good progress. None have been demonstrated to scale yet: if they had, we wouldnât have a couple of years left. But these approaches are much closer now, especially neutral atoms. To ignore this progress, youâd have to believe that every single approach will hit a wall.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Error correction.&lt;/b&gt; All quantum computers are noisy and require error-correcting codes to perform meaningful computation. This adds quite a bit of overhead, though how much depends on the architecture. More noise requires more error correction, but more interestingly, improved qubit connectivity allows for much more efficient codes. For a sense of scale: typically around a thousand physical qubits are required for one logical qubit for the superconducting quantum computers that are noisy and only have neighbor qubit connectivity. We knew âreconfigurable qubitsâ such as those of neutral-atom machines allow for an order of magnitude better error-correcting codes. Surprisingly, Oratomic showed the advantage is even larger: only about 3-4 physical neutral atom qubits are required per logical qubit.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Software. &lt;/b&gt;Lastly, the quantum algorithms to crack cryptography can be improved. This is Googleâs breakthrough: they massively sped up the algorithm to crack P-256. On top of that, Oratomic showed further architecture specific optimizations for reconfigurable qubits.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The picture comes together: in 2025 neutral atoms turned out to be more scalable than expected, and now Oratomic figured out how to do much better error-correcting codes with such highly connected qubits. On top of that, breaking P-256 requires much less work. The result is that Q-Day has been pulled forward significantly from typical &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/pq-2025/#is-q-day-always-fifteen-years-away\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;2035+ timelines&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, with neutral atoms in the lead, and other approaches not far behind.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;In previous blog posts &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/pq-2025/#progress-on-quantum-hardware\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;weâve discussed&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; how different quantum computers compare on physical qubit count and fidelity, compared to the conservative goalpost of cracking RSA-2048 on a superconducting qubit architecture. This analysis gives us a rough idea of how much time we have, and itâs certainly better than tracking &lt;a href=\&quot;https://bas.westerbaan.name/notes/2026/04/02/factoring.html\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;quantum factoring records&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, but it misses architecture-specific optimization and software improvements. What to watch for now is when the final missing &lt;a href=\&quot;https://westerbaan.name/~bas/rwpqc2026/adam.pdf\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;capabilities&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for each architecture are achieved.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;its-time-to-focus-on-authentication\&quot;&gt;Itâs time to focus on authentication&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#its-time-to-focus-on-authentication\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Historically, the industryâs focus on post-quantum cryptography (PQC) has been based largely on PQ &lt;i&gt;encryption, &lt;/i&gt;which stops harvest-now/decrypt-later (HNDL) attacks. In an HNDL attack, an adversary harvests sensitive encrypted network traffic today and stores it until a future date when it can use a powerful quantum computer to decrypt the data. HNDL attacks are the primary threat when Q-Day is far away. Thatâs why our focus, thus far, has been on mitigating this risk, by adopting post-quantum encryption by default in our products &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/post-quantum-for-all/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;since 2022&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Today, as we mentioned above, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/ssl/post-quantum-cryptography/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;most Cloudflare products&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; are secure against HNDL attacks, and weâre working to upgrade the rest as we speak.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The other category of attacks is against authentication: adversaries armed with functioning quantum computers impersonate servers or forge access credentials. If Q-Day is far off, authentication is not urgent: deploying PQ certificates and signatures does not add any value, only effort.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;An imminent Q-Day flips the script: data leaks are severe, but broken authentication is catastrophic. Any overlooked quantum-vulnerable remote-login key is an access point for an attacker to do as they wish, whether thatâs to extort, take down, or snoop on your system. Any automatic software-update mechanism becomes a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/security/what-is-remote-code-execution/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;remote code execution&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; vector. An active quantum attacker has it easy â they only need to find one trusted quantum-vulnerable key to get in.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;When experts in the field of building quantum computers start patching authentication systems, we should all listen. The question is no longer &amp;quot;when will our encrypted data be at risk?&amp;quot; but &amp;quot;how long before an attacker walks in the front door with a quantum-forged key?&amp;quot;&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;prioritizing-the-most-vulnerable-systems\&quot;&gt;Prioritizing the most vulnerable systems&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#prioritizing-the-most-vulnerable-systems\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;If quantum computers arrive in the next few years, they will be scarce and expensive. Attackers will prioritize high-value targets, like long-lived keys that unlock substantial assets or persistent access such as root certificates, API auth keys and code-signing certs. If an attacker is able to compromise one such key, they retain indefinite access until they are discovered or that key is revoked.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This suggests long-lived keys should be prioritized. That is certainly true if the quantum attack of a single key is expensive and slow, which is to be expected for the first generation of neutral atom quantum computers. Thatâs not the case for scalable superconducting quantum computers and later generations of neutral atom quantum computers, which could well crack keys much faster. Such fast CRQCs flip the script again, and an adversary with one might focus purely on HNDL attacks so that their attacks remain undetected. Googleâs Sophie Schmieg &lt;a href=\&quot;https://westerbaan.name/~bas/rwpqc2026/sophie.pdf\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;compares&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; this scenario to Enigmaâs cryptanalysis that changed the direction of World War II.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Adding support for PQ cryptography is not enough. Systems must disable support for quantum-vulnerable cryptography to be secure against &lt;a href=\&quot;https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downgrade_attack\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;downgrade attacks&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. In larger, especially federated systems such as the web, this is not feasible because not every client (browser) will support post-quantum certificates, and servers need to keep supporting these legacy clients. However, downgrade protection for HTTPS is still achievable using â&lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.chromium.org/Home/chromium-security/post-quantum-auth-roadmap\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;PQ HSTS&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;â and/or &lt;a href=\&quot;https://westerbaan.name/~bas/rwpqc2026/bas.pdf\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;certificate transparency&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Disabling quantum-vulnerable cryptography is not the last step: once done, all secrets such as passwords and access tokens previously exposed in the quantum-vulnerable system need to be rotated. Unlike post-quantum encryption, which takes one big push, migrating to post-quantum authentication has a long dependency chain â not to mention third-party validation and fraud monitoring. This will take years, not months.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Itâs natural for organizations reading this to rush out and think about which internal systems they need to upgrade. But thatâs not the end of the story. Q-day threatens all systems. As such, itâs important to understand the impact of a potential Q-day on third-party dependencies, both direct and indirect. Not just the third-parties you speak cryptography to, but also any third parties that are critical business dependencies like financial services and utilities.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;With Q-day approaching on a shorter timeline, post-quantum authentication is top priority. Long-term keys should be upgraded first. Deep dependency chains and the fact that everyone has third-party vendors means this effort will take on the order of years, not months. Upgrading to post-quantum cryptography is not enough: to prevent downgrades, quantum-vulnerable cryptography must also be turned off.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;cloudflares-roadmap-to-full-post-quantum-security\&quot;&gt;Cloudflareâs roadmap to full post-quantum security&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#cloudflares-roadmap-to-full-post-quantum-security\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Today, Cloudflare provides post-quantum encryption for the majority of our products mitigating harvest-now/decrypt-later. This is the product of work we started &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/introducing-universal-ssl/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;over a decade ago&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to protect our customers and the Internet at large.\n\nWe are targeting full post-quantum security including authentication for our entire product suite by 2029. Here weâre sharing some intermediate milestones weâve set, subject to change as our understanding of the risk and deployment challenges evolve.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/CxLrmxb2dExy2S5FL0ouy/e1ee5616ce31d15ed8e0fd7b40ae2a39/image2.jpg\&quot; alt=\&quot;Mid-2026: PQ authentication (ML-DSA) support for Cloudflare â origin connections. Mid-2027: PQ authentication support for visitor â Cloudflare connections using Merkle Tree Certificates. Early 2028: Cloudflare One SASE suite adds PQ authentication to make it fully PQ secure. 2029: Cloudflare fully post-quantum secure.\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1520\&quot; height=\&quot;850\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;what-we-recommend\&quot;&gt;What we recommend&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#what-we-recommend\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;For businesses, we recommend making post-quantum support a requirement for any procurement. Common best practices, like keeping software updated and automating certificate issuance, are meaningful and will get you pretty far. We recommend assessing critical vendors early for what their failure to take action would mean for your business.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For regulatory agencies and governments: leading by setting early timelines has been crucial for industry-wide progress so far. We are now in a pivotal position where fragmentation in standards and effort between and within jurisdictions could put progress at risk. We recommend that governments assign and empower a lead agency to coordinate the migration on a clear timeline, stay security-focused, and promote the use of existing international standards. Governments need not panic, but can lead migration with confidence.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For Cloudflare customers, with respect to our services, you do not need to take any mitigating action. We are following the latest advancements in quantum computing closely and taking proactive steps to protect your data. As we have done in the past, we will turn on post-quantum security by default, with no switches to flip. What we donât control is the other side: browsers, applications, and origins need to upgrade. Corporate network traffic on Cloudflare need not worry: Cloudflare One offers &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/post-quantum-sase/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;end-to-end protection&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; when tunnelling traffic through our post-quantum encrypted infrastructure.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Privacy and security are table stakes for the Internet. That&amp;#39;s why every post-quantum upgrade we build will continue to be available to all customers, on every plan, at &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/post-quantum-crypto-should-be-free/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;no additional cost&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Making post-quantum security the default is the only way to protect the Internet at scale.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/introducing-universal-ssl/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Free TLS&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; helped encrypt the web. Free post-quantum cryptography will help secure it for what comes next.&lt;/p&gt;&quot;],&quot;published_at&quot;:[0,&quot;2026-04-07T22:00+01:00&quot;],&quot;updated_at&quot;:[0,&quot;2026-04-15T08:32:00.483Z&quot;],&quot;feature_image&quot;:[0,&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/6siStuS5VWsIdEag8FQZkF/2e014e951328beb480468dffda783c2d/image1.png&quot;],&quot;tags&quot;:[1,[[0,{&quot;id&quot;:[0,&quot;6bIo7ayy56Fzdrtf9z2EWy&quot;],&quot;name&quot;:[0,&quot;Post-Quantum&quot;],&quot;slug&quot;:[0,&quot;post-quantum&quot;]}],[0,{&quot;id&quot;:[0,&quot;6Mp7ouACN2rT3YjL1xaXJx&quot;],&quot;name&quot;:[0,&quot;Security&quot;],&quot;slug&quot;:[0,&quot;security&quot;]}]]],&quot;relatedTags&quot;:[0],&quot;authors&quot;:[1,[[0,{&quot;name&quot;:[0,&quot;Bas Westerbaan&quot;],&quot;slug&quot;:[0,&quot;bas&quot;],&quot;bio&quot;:[0,null],&quot;profile_image&quot;:[0,&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/4KeBG8XO1ADZHuEV9v5Hwz/6c90a96cfb1373b208bffc35f3fd71f4/bas.png&quot;],&quot;location&quot;:[0,&quot;The Netherlands&quot;],&quot;website&quot;:[0,&quot;https://bas.westerbaan.name&quot;],&quot;twitter&quot;:[0,&quot;@bwesterb&quot;],&quot;facebook&quot;:[0,null],&quot;publiclyIndex&quot;:[0,true]}]]],&quot;meta_description&quot;:[0,&quot;Recent advances in quantum hardware and software have accelerated the timeline on which quantum attack might happen. component-export="Post" renderer-url="/_astro/client.Ck_OXNAA.js" props="{&quot;post&quot;:[0,{&quot;id&quot;:[0,&quot;1vlCZshPEwWazkAnJsxN44&quot;],&quot;title&quot;:[0,&quot;Cloudflare targets 2029 for full post-quantum security&quot;],&quot;slug&quot;:[0,&quot;post-quantum-roadmap&quot;],&quot;excerpt&quot;:[0,&quot;Recent advances in quantum hardware and software have accelerated the timeline on which quantum attack might happen. Cloudflare is responding by moving our target for full post-quantum security to 2029.&quot;],&quot;featured&quot;:[0,false],&quot;html&quot;:[0,&quot;&lt;p&gt;Cloudflare is accelerating its post-quantum roadmap. We now target &lt;b&gt;2029&lt;/b&gt; to be fully post-quantum (PQ) secure including, crucially, post-quantum authentication.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;At Cloudflare, we believe in making the Internet private and secure by default. We started by offering &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/introducing-universal-ssl/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;free universal SSL certificates&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; in 2014, began preparing our &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/towards-post-quantum-cryptography-in-tls/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;post-quantum migration&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; in 2019, and enabled post-quantum encryption for &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/post-quantum-for-all/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;all websites&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; and APIs in 2022, mitigating harvest-now/decrypt-later attacks. While weâre excited by the fact that over &lt;a href=\&quot;https://radar.cloudflare.com/post-quantum\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;65% of human traffic&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to Cloudflare is post-quantum encrypted, our work is not done until authentication is also upgraded. Credible new research and rapid industry developments suggest that the deadline to migrate is much sooner than expected. This is a challenge that any organization must treat with urgency, which is why weâre expediting our own internal Q-Day readiness timeline.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;What happened? Last week, Google &lt;a href=\&quot;https://research.google/blog/safeguarding-cryptocurrency-by-disclosing-quantum-vulnerabilities-responsibly/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;announced&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; they had drastically improved upon the quantum algorithm to break elliptic curve cryptography, which is widely used to secure the Internet. They did not reveal the algorithm, but instead provided a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zero-knowledge_proof\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;zero-knowledge proof&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; that they have one.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This is not even the biggest breakthrough. That same day, Oratomic &lt;a href=\&quot;https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.28627\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;published&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; a resource estimate for breaking RSA-2048 and P-256 on a neutral atom computer. For P-256, it only requires a shockingly low 10,000 qubits. Googleâs motivation behind their recent announcement to also pursue &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.google/innovation-and-ai/technology/research/neutral-atom-quantum-computers/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;neutral atoms&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; alongside superconducting quantum computers becomes clear now. Although Oratomic explains their basic approach, they still leave out crucial details &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.oratomic.com/news/responsible-disclosure-in-the-era-of-quantum-computers\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;on purpose&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;These independent advances prompted Google to accelerate their post-quantum migration timeline to &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.google/innovation-and-ai/technology/safety-security/cryptography-migration-timeline/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;2029&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Whatâs more, in their announcement and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://westerbaan.name/~bas/rwpqc2026/sophie.pdf\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;other talks&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, Google has placed a priority on quantum-secure authentication over mitigating harvest-now/decrypt-later attacks. As we discuss next, this priority indicates that Google is concerned about Q-Day coming as soon as 2030. Following the announcements, IBM Quantum Safeâs CTO is more pessimistic and canât rule out quantum âmoonshot attacksâ on high value targets &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.linkedin.com/pulse/quantum-timeline-elliptic-curve-cryptography-just-jumped-osborne-k1oae\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;as early as 2029&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The quantum threat is well known: Q-Day is the day that sufficiently capable quantum computers can break essential cryptography used to protect data and access across systems today.&lt;i&gt; &lt;/i&gt;Cryptographically relevant quantum computers (CRQCs) donât exist yet, but many labs across the world are pursuing different approaches to building one. Until recently, progress on CRQCs has been mostly public, but there is no reason to expect that will continue. Indeed, there is ample reason to expect that progress will leave the public eye. As quantum computer scientist Scott Aaronson &lt;a href=\&quot;https://scottaaronson.blog/?p=9425\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;warned&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; at the end of 2025:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;blockquote&gt;&lt;p&gt;[A]t some point, the people doing detailed estimates of how many physical qubits and gates itâll take to break actually deployed cryptosystems using Shorâs algorithm are going to stop publishing those estimates, if for no other reason than the risk of giving too much information to adversaries. Indeed, for all we know, that point may have been passed already.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/blockquote&gt;&lt;p&gt;That point has now passed indeed.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;why-now-independent-progress-on-three-fronts\&quot;&gt;Why now: independent progress on three fronts&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#why-now-independent-progress-on-three-fronts\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Weâd like to spend some words on why itâs difficult to predict progress on quantum computing. Sudden âquantumâ leaps in understanding, like the one we witnessed last week, can occur even if everything happens in the public eye. Simply put, breaking cryptography with a quantum computer requires engineering on three independent fronts: quantum hardware, error correction, and quantum software. Progress on each front compounds progress on the others.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Hardware.&lt;/b&gt; There are many different competing approaches. We mentioned neutral atoms and superconducting qubits, but there are also ion-trap, photonics, and moonshots like topological qubits. Complementary approaches can even be &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.caltech.edu/about/news/low-noise-transducers-to-bridge-the-gap-between-microwave-and-optical-qubits\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;combined&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Most of these approaches are pursued by several labs around the world. They all have their distinct engineering challenges and problems to solve before they can scale up. A few years ago, all of them had a long list of open challenges, and it was unclear if any of them would scale. Today most of them have made good progress. None have been demonstrated to scale yet: if they had, we wouldnât have a couple of years left. But these approaches are much closer now, especially neutral atoms. To ignore this progress, youâd have to believe that every single approach will hit a wall.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Error correction.&lt;/b&gt; All quantum computers are noisy and require error-correcting codes to perform meaningful computation. This adds quite a bit of overhead, though how much depends on the architecture. More noise requires more error correction, but more interestingly, improved qubit connectivity allows for much more efficient codes. For a sense of scale: typically around a thousand physical qubits are required for one logical qubit for the superconducting quantum computers that are noisy and only have neighbor qubit connectivity. We knew âreconfigurable qubitsâ such as those of neutral-atom machines allow for an order of magnitude better error-correcting codes. Surprisingly, Oratomic showed the advantage is even larger: only about 3-4 physical neutral atom qubits are required per logical qubit.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Software. &lt;/b&gt;Lastly, the quantum algorithms to crack cryptography can be improved. This is Googleâs breakthrough: they massively sped up the algorithm to crack P-256. On top of that, Oratomic showed further architecture specific optimizations for reconfigurable qubits.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The picture comes together: in 2025 neutral atoms turned out to be more scalable than expected, and now Oratomic figured out how to do much better error-correcting codes with such highly connected qubits. On top of that, breaking P-256 requires much less work. The result is that Q-Day has been pulled forward significantly from typical &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/pq-2025/#is-q-day-always-fifteen-years-away\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;2035+ timelines&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, with neutral atoms in the lead, and other approaches not far behind.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;In previous blog posts &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/pq-2025/#progress-on-quantum-hardware\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;weâve discussed&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; how different quantum computers compare on physical qubit count and fidelity, compared to the conservative goalpost of cracking RSA-2048 on a superconducting qubit architecture. This analysis gives us a rough idea of how much time we have, and itâs certainly better than tracking &lt;a href=\&quot;https://bas.westerbaan.name/notes/2026/04/02/factoring.html\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;quantum factoring records&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, but it misses architecture-specific optimization and software improvements. What to watch for now is when the final missing &lt;a href=\&quot;https://westerbaan.name/~bas/rwpqc2026/adam.pdf\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;capabilities&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for each architecture are achieved.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;its-time-to-focus-on-authentication\&quot;&gt;Itâs time to focus on authentication&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#its-time-to-focus-on-authentication\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Historically, the industryâs focus on post-quantum cryptography (PQC) has been based largely on PQ &lt;i&gt;encryption, &lt;/i&gt;which stops harvest-now/decrypt-later (HNDL) attacks. In an HNDL attack, an adversary harvests sensitive encrypted network traffic today and stores it until a future date when it can use a powerful quantum computer to decrypt the data. HNDL attacks are the primary threat when Q-Day is far away. Thatâs why our focus, thus far, has been on mitigating this risk, by adopting post-quantum encryption by default in our products &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/post-quantum-for-all/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;since 2022&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Today, as we mentioned above, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/ssl/post-quantum-cryptography/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;most Cloudflare products&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; are secure against HNDL attacks, and weâre working to upgrade the rest as we speak.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The other category of attacks is against authentication: adversaries armed with functioning quantum computers impersonate servers or forge access credentials. If Q-Day is far off, authentication is not urgent: deploying PQ certificates and signatures does not add any value, only effort.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;An imminent Q-Day flips the script: data leaks are severe, but broken authentication is catastrophic. Any overlooked quantum-vulnerable remote-login key is an access point for an attacker to do as they wish, whether thatâs to extort, take down, or snoop on your system. Any automatic software-update mechanism becomes a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/security/what-is-remote-code-execution/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;remote code execution&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; vector. An active quantum attacker has it easy â they only need to find one trusted quantum-vulnerable key to get in.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;When experts in the field of building quantum computers start patching authentication systems, we should all listen. The question is no longer &amp;quot;when will our encrypted data be at risk?&amp;quot; but &amp;quot;how long before an attacker walks in the front door with a quantum-forged key?&amp;quot;&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;prioritizing-the-most-vulnerable-systems\&quot;&gt;Prioritizing the most vulnerable systems&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#prioritizing-the-most-vulnerable-systems\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;If quantum computers arrive in the next few years, they will be scarce and expensive. Attackers will prioritize high-value targets, like long-lived keys that unlock substantial assets or persistent access such as root certificates, API auth keys and code-signing certs. If an attacker is able to compromise one such key, they retain indefinite access until they are discovered or that key is revoked.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This suggests long-lived keys should be prioritized. That is certainly true if the quantum attack of a single key is expensive and slow, which is to be expected for the first generation of neutral atom quantum computers. Thatâs not the case for scalable superconducting quantum computers and later generations of neutral atom quantum computers, which could well crack keys much faster. Such fast CRQCs flip the script again, and an adversary with one might focus purely on HNDL attacks so that their attacks remain undetected. Googleâs Sophie Schmieg &lt;a href=\&quot;https://westerbaan.name/~bas/rwpqc2026/sophie.pdf\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;compares&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; this scenario to Enigmaâs cryptanalysis that changed the direction of World War II.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Adding support for PQ cryptography is not enough. Systems must disable support for quantum-vulnerable cryptography to be secure against &lt;a href=\&quot;https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downgrade_attack\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;downgrade attacks&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. In larger, especially federated systems such as the web, this is not feasible because not every client (browser) will support post-quantum certificates, and servers need to keep supporting these legacy clients. However, downgrade protection for HTTPS is still achievable using â&lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.chromium.org/Home/chromium-security/post-quantum-auth-roadmap\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;PQ HSTS&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;â and/or &lt;a href=\&quot;https://westerbaan.name/~bas/rwpqc2026/bas.pdf\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;certificate transparency&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Disabling quantum-vulnerable cryptography is not the last step: once done, all secrets such as passwords and access tokens previously exposed in the quantum-vulnerable system need to be rotated. Unlike post-quantum encryption, which takes one big push, migrating to post-quantum authentication has a long dependency chain â not to mention third-party validation and fraud monitoring. This will take years, not months.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Itâs natural for organizations reading this to rush out and think about which internal systems they need to upgrade. But thatâs not the end of the story. Q-day threatens all systems. As such, itâs important to understand the impact of a potential Q-day on third-party dependencies, both direct and indirect. Not just the third-parties you speak cryptography to, but also any third parties that are critical business dependencies like financial services and utilities.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;With Q-day approaching on a shorter timeline, post-quantum authentication is top priority. Long-term keys should be upgraded first. Deep dependency chains and the fact that everyone has third-party vendors means this effort will take on the order of years, not months. Upgrading to post-quantum cryptography is not enough: to prevent downgrades, quantum-vulnerable cryptography must also be turned off.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;cloudflares-roadmap-to-full-post-quantum-security\&quot;&gt;Cloudflareâs roadmap to full post-quantum security&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#cloudflares-roadmap-to-full-post-quantum-security\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Today, Cloudflare provides post-quantum encryption for the majority of our products mitigating harvest-now/decrypt-later. This is the product of work we started &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/introducing-universal-ssl/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;over a decade ago&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to protect our customers and the Internet at large.\n\nWe are targeting full post-quantum security including authentication for our entire product suite by 2029. Here weâre sharing some intermediate milestones weâve set, subject to change as our understanding of the risk and deployment challenges evolve.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/CxLrmxb2dExy2S5FL0ouy/e1ee5616ce31d15ed8e0fd7b40ae2a39/image2.jpg\&quot; alt=\&quot;Mid-2026: PQ authentication (ML-DSA) support for Cloudflare â origin connections. Mid-2027: PQ authentication support for visitor â Cloudflare connections using Merkle Tree Certificates. Early 2028: Cloudflare One SASE suite adds PQ authentication to make it fully PQ secure. 2029: Cloudflare fully post-quantum secure.\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1520\&quot; height=\&quot;850\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;what-we-recommend\&quot;&gt;What we recommend&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#what-we-recommend\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;For businesses, we recommend making post-quantum support a requirement for any procurement. Common best practices, like keeping software updated and automating certificate issuance, are meaningful and will get you pretty far. We recommend assessing critical vendors early for what their failure to take action would mean for your business.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For regulatory agencies and governments: leading by setting early timelines has been crucial for industry-wide progress so far. We are now in a pivotal position where fragmentation in standards and effort between and within jurisdictions could put progress at risk. We recommend that governments assign and empower a lead agency to coordinate the migration on a clear timeline, stay security-focused, and promote the use of existing international standards. Governments need not panic, but can lead migration with confidence.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For Cloudflare customers, with respect to our services, you do not need to take any mitigating action. We are following the latest advancements in quantum computing closely and taking proactive steps to protect your data. As we have done in the past, we will turn on post-quantum security by default, with no switches to flip. What we donât control is the other side: browsers, applications, and origins need to upgrade. Corporate network traffic on Cloudflare need not worry: Cloudflare One offers &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/post-quantum-sase/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;end-to-end protection&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; when tunnelling traffic through our post-quantum encrypted infrastructure.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Privacy and security are table stakes for the Internet. That&amp;#39;s why every post-quantum upgrade we build will continue to be available to all customers, on every plan, at &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/post-quantum-crypto-should-be-free/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;no additional cost&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Making post-quantum security the default is the only way to protect the Internet at scale.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/introducing-universal-ssl/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Free TLS&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; helped encrypt the web. Free post-quantum cryptography will help secure it for what comes next.&lt;/p&gt;&quot;],&quot;published_at&quot;:[0,&quot;2026-04-07T22:00+01:00&quot;],&quot;updated_at&quot;:[0,&quot;2026-04-15T08:32:00.483Z&quot;],&quot;feature_image&quot;:[0,&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/6siStuS5VWsIdEag8FQZkF/2e014e951328beb480468dffda783c2d/image1.png&quot;],&quot;tags&quot;:[1,[[0,{&quot;id&quot;:[0,&quot;6bIo7ayy56Fzdrtf9z2EWy&quot;],&quot;name&quot;:[0,&quot;Post-Quantum&quot;],&quot;slug&quot;:[0,&quot;post-quantum&quot;]}],[0,{&quot;id&quot;:[0,&quot;6Mp7ouACN2rT3YjL1xaXJx&quot;],&quot;name&quot;:[0,&quot;Security&quot;],&quot;slug&quot;:[0,&quot;security&quot;]}]]],&quot;relatedTags&quot;:[0],&quot;authors&quot;:[1,[[0,{&quot;name&quot;:[0,&quot;Bas Westerbaan&quot;],&quot;slug&quot;:[0,&quot;bas&quot;],&quot;bio&quot;:[0,null],&quot;profile_image&quot;:[0,&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/4KeBG8XO1ADZHuEV9v5Hwz/6c90a96cfb1373b208bffc35f3fd71f4/bas.png&quot;],&quot;location&quot;:[0,&quot;The Netherlands&quot;],&quot;website&quot;:[0,&quot;https://bas.westerbaan.name&quot;],&quot;twitter&quot;:[0,&quot;@bwesterb&quot;],&quot;facebook&quot;:[0,null],&quot;publiclyIndex&quot;:[0,true]}]]],&quot;meta_description&quot;:[0,&quot;Recent advances in quantum hardware and software have accelerated the timeline on which quantum attack might happen. These tools help developers implement a true least-privilege architecture while protecting against credential leakage.\n&quot;],&quot;featured&quot;:[0,false],&quot;html&quot;:[0,&quot;&lt;p&gt;Agents let you build software faster than ever, but securing your environment and the code you write â from both mistakes and malice â takes real effort. &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/security/threats/owasp-top-10/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Open Web Application Security Project&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; (OWASP) details a number of &lt;a href=\&quot;https://genai.owasp.org/resource/agentic-ai-threats-and-mitigations/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;risks&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; present in agentic AI systems, including the risk of credential leaks, user impersonation, and elevation of privilege. These risks can result in extreme damage to your environments including denial of service, data loss, or data leaks â which can do untold financial and reputational damage.Â  &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This is an identity problem. In modern development, &amp;quot;identities&amp;quot; aren&amp;#39;t just people â they are the agents, scripts, and third-party tools that act on your behalf. To secure these non-human identities, you need to manage their entire lifecycle: ensuring their credentials (tokens) aren&amp;#39;t leaked, seeing which applications have access via OAuth, and narrowing their permissions using granular RBAC.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Today, we are introducing updates to address these needs: scannable tokens to protect your credentials,&lt;b&gt; &lt;/b&gt;OAuth visibility to manage your principals, and resource-scoped RBAC to fine-tune your policies.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;understanding-identity-principals-credentials-and-policies\&quot;&gt;Understanding identity: Principals, Credentials, and Policies&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#understanding-identity-principals-credentials-and-policies\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;To secure the Internet in an era of &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/ai/what-is-agentic-ai/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;autonomous agents&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, we have to rethink how we handle identity. Whether a request comes from a human developer or an AI agent, every interaction with an API relies on three core pillars:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;The Principal (The Traveler):&lt;/b&gt; This is the identity itself â the &amp;quot;who.&amp;quot; It might be you logging in via OAuth, or a background agent using an API token to deploy code.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;The Credential (The Passport):&lt;/b&gt; This is the proof of that identity. In this world, your API token is your passport. If itâs stolen or leaked, anyone can &amp;quot;wear&amp;quot; your identity.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;The Policy (The Visa):&lt;/b&gt; This defines what that identity is allowed to do. Just because you have a valid passport doesn&amp;#39;t mean you have a visa to enter every country. A policy ensures that even a verified identity can only access the specific resources it needs.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;When these three pillars aren&amp;#39;t managed together, security breaks down. You might have a valid Principal using a stolen Credential, or a legitimate identity with a Policy that is far too broad. &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;leaked-token-detection\&quot;&gt;Leaked token detection&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#leaked-token-detection\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Agents and other third-party applications use API tokens to access the Cloudflare API. One of the simplest ways that we see people leaking their secrets is by accidentally pushing them to a public GitHub repository. &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.gitguardian.com/files/the-state-of-secrets-sprawl-report-2026\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;GitGuardian&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; reports that last year more than 28 million secrets were published to public GitHub repositories, and that AI is causing leaks to happen 5x faster than before.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;If an API token is a digital passport, then leaking it on a public repository is like leaving your passport on a park bench. Anyone who finds it can impersonate that identity until the document is canceled. Our partnership with GitHub acts like a global &amp;quot;lost and found&amp;quot; for these credentials. By the time you realize your passport is missing, weâve already identified the document, verified its authenticity via the checksum, and voided it to prevent misuse.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Weâre partnering with several leading credential scanning tools to help proactively find your leaked tokens and revoke them before they could be used maliciously. We know itâs not a matter of if, but rather when, before you, an employee, or one of your agents makes a mistake and pushes a secret somewhere it shouldnât be.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;github\&quot;&gt;GitHub&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#github\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Weâve partnered with GitHub and are participating in their Secret Scanning program to find your tokens in both public and private repositories. By the time you realize you made a mistake, we&amp;#39;ve already fixed it.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We hope this is the kind of feature you donât need to use, but our partners are on the lookout for leaks to help keep you secure.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;cloudflare-one\&quot;&gt;Cloudflare One&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#cloudflare-one\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Cloudflare One customers are also protected from these leaks. A token in a file upload, an outbound request, or a download is stopped before it reaches its destination.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Outbound Email (&lt;/b&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/sase/products/email-security/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;b&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Email Security&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/a&gt;&lt;b&gt;):&lt;/b&gt; Microsoft 365 customers can extend this same prevention to Outlook. The &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/email-security/outbound-dlp/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;DLP Assist&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; The âcfâ prefix makes Cloudflare tokens instantly recognizable with greater confidence, and the checksum makes it easy for tools to statically validate them. Your existing tokens will continue to work, but every new token you generate will use the scannable format so itâs easily detected with high confidence.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;table&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Credential Type&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;What it&amp;#39;s for&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;New Format&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;User API Key&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Legacy global API key tied to your user account (full access)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;cfk_[40 characters][checksum]&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;User API Token&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Scoped token you create for specific permissions&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;cfut_[40 characters][checksum]&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;tr&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Account API Token&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;Token owned by the account (not a specific user)&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;td&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;cfat_[40 characters][checksum]&lt;/b&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/td&gt;&lt;/tr&gt;&lt;/table&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;getting-started\&quot;&gt;Getting started&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#getting-started\&quot; Understanding these scopes before you grant access helps you maintain least-privilege. &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We also added a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://dash.cloudflare.com/profile/access-management/authorization\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Connected Applications&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; experience so you can see which applications have access to which accounts, what scopes/permissions are associated with that application, and easily revoke that access as needed.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/5Aiu82urkjaL9SWpZUBgNi/827cf38aa655d4094de1895d07f51137/BLOG-3216_5.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;BLOG-3216 5\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1530\&quot; height=\&quot;527\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;getting-started\&quot;&gt;Getting started&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#getting-started\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The OAuth consent and revocation improvements are available now. Check which apps currently have access to your accounts by visiting My Profile &amp;gt; Access Management &amp;gt; Connected Applications.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For developers building integrations with Cloudflare, keep an eye on the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/changelog\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Changelog&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for more announcements around how you can register your own OAuth apps soon!Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;fine-grained-resource-level-permissioning\&quot;&gt;Fine-grained resource-level permissioningÂ &lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#fine-grained-resource-level-permissioning\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;If the token is the passport, then resource-scoped permissions are the visas inside it. Having a valid passport gets you through the front door, but it shouldn&amp;#39;t give you access to every room in the building. By narrowing the scope to specific resources â like a single Load Balancer pool or a specific Gateway policy â you are ensuring that even if an identity is verified, it only has the &amp;quot;visa&amp;quot; to go where itâs strictly necessary.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Last year, we &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/changelog/post/2025-10-01-fine-grained-permissioning-beta/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;announced&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; support for resource scoped permissions in Cloudflareâs &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/access-management/role-based-access-control-rbac/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;role-based access control (RBAC)&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; system for several of our Zero Trust products. This enables you to right size permissions for both users and agents to minimize security risks. Weâve expanded this capability to several new resources-level permissions. The resource scope is now supported for:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access Applications&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access Identity Providers&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access Policies&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access Service Tokens&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Access Targets&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;Weâve also completely overhauled the API Token creation experience, making it easier for customers to provision and manage Account API Tokens right from the Cloudflare Dashboard. &lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/2XSjOtE46g8iVNN7QNzoDI/2f2280b106da9ebc9f3959d5300a4241/account_owned_token_gif.gif\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1280\&quot; height=\&quot;659\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h4 id=\&quot;how-it-works\&quot;&gt;How it works&lt;/h4&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#how-it-works\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;When you add a member to your Cloudflare account or create an API Token, you typically assign that principal a policy. A Permission Policy is what gives a principal permission to take an action, whether thatâs managing Cloudflare One Access Applications, or DNS Records. Without a policy, a principal can authenticate, but they are unauthorized to do any actions within an account.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Policies are made up of three components: a Principal, a Role, and a Scope. The Principal is who or what you&amp;#39;re granting access to, whether that&amp;#39;s a human user, a Non-Human Identity (NHI) like an API Token, or increasingly, an Agent acting on behalf of a user. The Role defines what actions they&amp;#39;re permitted to take. The Scope determines where those permissions apply, and historically, that&amp;#39;s been restricted to the entire account, or individual zones.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;new-permission-roles\&quot;&gt;New permission roles&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#new-permission-roles\&quot; By refining how we manage permissions and credentials, developers and enterprises can have greater confidence in their security posture across the users, apps, agents, and scripts that access Cloudflare. Least privilege isnât a new concept, and for enterprises, itâs never been optional. Whether a human administrator is managing a zone or an agent is programmatically deploying a Worker, the expectation is the same, they should only be authorized to do the job it was given, and nothing else.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Following todayâs announcement, we recommend customers:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ol&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;Review your &lt;a This shift has moved well beyond our engineering organization, with employees across product, sales, marketing, and finance teams now using agentic workflows to drive efficiency in their daily tasks. But the adoption of agentic workflow with MCP is not without its security risks. These range from authorization sprawl, &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/ai/prompt-injection/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;prompt injection&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/learning/security/what-is-a-supply-chain-attack/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;supply chain risks&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. To secure this broad company-wide adoption, we have integrated a suite of security controls from both our &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/sase/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare One (SASE) platform&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; and our &lt;a href=\&quot;https://workers.cloudflare.com/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Developer platform&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, allowing us to govern AI usage with MCP without slowing down our workforce.Â  &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;In this blog weâll walk through our own best practices for securing MCP workflows, by putting different parts of our platform together to create a unified security architecture for the era of autonomous AI. Weâll also share two new concepts that support enterprise MCP deployments:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;We are launching &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/access-controls/ai-controls/mcp-portals/#code-mode\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Code Mode with MCP server portals&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, to drastically reduce token costs associated with MCP usage;Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;We describe how to use &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-wan/zero-trust/cloudflare-gateway/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Gateway&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for Shadow MCP detection, to discover use of unauthorized remote MCP servers.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;We also talk about how our organization approached deploying MCP, and how we built out our MCP security architecture using Cloudflare products including &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/agents/guides/remote-mcp-server/\&quot;
